# DevThink release notes

## 2.0.3 — the prisma context home, the boundary completed, the release lane of the family

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| The prisma context home | The whole prisma context answers the web workbench the doctrine owns: the repository root carries no prisma folder anymore — the local sqlite home of the gateway lineage moves inside web/ (web/prisma/, the marker keeps the directory present so a fresh checkout resolves the local file urls without a bootstrap step), the schema stays canonical at web/schema.prisma, and every default url (prisma.config.ts, database.ts, the scaffolded templates of server.ts, the .env example, the engine silent test database) resolves file:./web/prisma/devthink.db against the new home. The scaffold creates web/prisma/ beside web/schema.prisma for the consumer, the gitignore tracks the db artifacts at their new path, and the doc trees (the gateway console page, the type documentation) draw the folder the consumer will actually see. |
| The boundary completed | The interior audit of the root surface closes the interface-versus-logic doctrine the 2.0.2 release opened: the ansi render family of ui.ts (banner, box, status bar, spinner, the format helpers — pure terminal drawing, zero business logic) merges into web/terminal-ui.tsx where the design of the whole project lives, the command family imports the drawing from the design room, and the legacy ui.ts dissolves; the glyph and destination vocabulary of workspace.ts crosses the same way (every consumer already lived in the web pages). The root keeps the logic the library owns and the web owns the drawing — the direction the doctrine pinned: the web imports the root, the root never imports the web except through the design room contract the terminal entry already carried. |
| The release lane of the family | The release workflow learns the auto-tag doctrine of the saddle line: a push to main that touches package.json and CHANGELOG.md resolves the bumped version against the latest existing tag, extracts the changelog section of the version (a version without a section refuses to ship — the release body contract), cuts the tag, archives the tagged tree at zip -9 under the family asset name and creates the release — the whole pipeline fires without a manual step, and the maintenance ladder that pre-cuts its own tags finds nothing left to cut (the version equals the tag, the detector skips). |
| The asset lanes | The four asset lanes of the rich distribution (the extension build, the container, the desktop binaries and the mobile shells) restore the workflow_run dual trigger the grand merge dropped: the release event the githubrelease job publishes with the repository token never reaches a workflow (the anti-recursion suppression the family documented), so each lane now listens to the completed DevThink Release workflow with the tag and conclusion guards, resolves the release tag from the live release when the event carries none, checks the tagged source out and attaches its artifacts on the automatic path — the sixteen assets of the 2.0.2 release needed a manual dispatch, the 2.0.3 chain assembles itself. |
| The publish lanes | The five registry lanes gain the conclusion guard (a failed release never fires a publish), the event-scoped concurrency groups (a push and its workflow_run twin run side by side, no lane cancels its sibling), the rubygems lane reads the node baseline from the .nvmrc the engines pin, and the npmjs lane degrades gracefully without NPM_TOKEN the way the family contract always stated (the token rides the environment, never an if condition). |
| The pin family | One version per action across the whole workflow corpus: setup-java answers v6.0.1 everywhere (the mobile and maven lanes carried v5.7.0, the verify lane v6.0.1), deploy-pages answers v5.0.1, the codeql action family answers v4.38.0 (the bare v4 the scorecard lane carried), and the runtime setups pin what they run (dotnet 10.0.112, ruby 3.4.10, the rust toolchain 1.120) — the mutable latest and stable the lanes carried never build the same artifact twice. |
| The envelope family | The metadata lockstep closes its gaps: DevThink.java and DevThinkCli.cs join the version stamps at 2.0.3 (they answered 2.0.0 since the merge — no gate watched them), the Dockerfile carries the version in every default the build args ride (the five places the 2.0.0 defaults stamped), the .npmrc answers the family engine policy (engine-strict false — the registry install of a consumer with a different toolchain never fails), and the extension remnants of the release machinery (the artifact catalog of release.mjs, the maven and nuget identities of pack.ts, the release notes tests) answer the devthink names the channels actually publish. The dead configs of the extension folder (the zero-byte netlify manifest, the vercel manifest, the capacitor json with the appId of the old identity) leave the tree — zero references, the mobile lane derives the real capacitor config from web/capacitor.config.ts. |
| The container hardening | The Dockerfile adopts the apt retry of the saddle standard: the update and the install retry together (five attempts, the mirror drift the family documented never fails a build), the comments of the pinned build stages drop the emulation talk the $BUILDPLATFORM pin retired, and the web netlify baseline answers the node line the .nvmrc pins (26.8.2 — the 22 the manifest carried predates the merge). |

### Fixed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| The typecheck of the root | The exactOptionalPropertyTypes discipline reaches the gateway pairing client: the context builder never assigns an explicit undefined to an optional property (the pre-generate error the whole-project typecheck carried since the merge, invisible to the build tsconfig the declaration emit rides) — and the biome config loses its duplicate suspicious block (the second key shadowed the first, the linter answered one error on its own config file). |
| The maintenance lane | The propose job of the maintenance ladder answers a real run every firing: outside its window it reports the no-op as a notice instead of skipping — the board carries green runs, never skipped jobs (the nothing-skipped doctrine). The cache retention of the cleaner and the maintenance sweep align on the family baseline (6h — the drift the two lanes carried). |
| The secrets check | The scanner gate answers its own rename: checksecrets.ts (the lowercase-no-hyphen naming doctrine) with every reference — the package script, the Dockerfile step and the internal file list — reading the same module. |

## 2.0.2 — the design room boundary, the freeze resync, the latent red of the dissolved envelope

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| The design room boundary | The interior of every root logic answers the interface-versus-logic doctrine: the browser gateway pairing client (gateway.ts) leaves the web design room for the repository root — the one piece of web that was logic all along (the pairing context, the token store, the fetch wrapper: pure TypeScript against the DOM surface, no design) — while the ink terminal workspace (terminal-ui.tsx) crosses the other way: the terminal design is design, so the component that draws the compact workspace grammar joins the web design room where the design of the whole project lives (the cli, the pages, the vercel and netlify surfaces, the tv and android shells). Six workbench pages (providers, console, routes, usage, settings, projects) import the gateway client from the root across the boundary, the devthink command family imports the terminal workspace from the design room, and the vite server of the workbench opens its file-system allow to the repository root so the dev server serves the shared sources the same way the build bundles them. The root tsconfig keeps the logic check, the web tsconfig typechecks the design with the shared logic it imports, and both answer green over the moved files. |
| The frozen contract resync | The 2.0.1 consolidation re-keyed the merged lineages behind devthink surfaces and the frozen contract hashes moved with the text: the audit event names, the frozen message catalog, the error code table and the permission coverage drifted from the pinned hashes without a release bump, so the verify gate refused the release candidate (the readiness review answered no-go at twenty-six of twenty-seven). This release is the bump the freeze doctrine demands: the version moves, the freeze artifact re-freezes at the release, the permission baseline re-records, and the ladder answers green again. |
| The metadata envelope catalog | The release metadata sync stops requiring the mobile package envelope the 2.0.1 consolidation dissolved: the mobile identity lives at the web root (web/capacitor.config.ts) and the shell builds in the mobile lane, so no mobile/package.json ever exists again — the sync catalog of required envelopes drops the entry and the latent red (the maintenance metadata gate would refuse every future sync over a file the standard retired) closes at the root. The maintenance ladder learns the container image clock: the official node images publish behind the nodejs.org releases, so the node baseline walks the stable line back to the newest release whose pinned bookworm-slim image exists — the 2.0.2 candidate stamped 26.8.1 when the registry carried no image of the 26.8.2 the index shipped, and the registry published the image hours later while the release lane verified the tag, so the baseline rides 26.8.2 the moment the image lands. The workflow renders compose per file so no pin entry resurrects the bytes a sibling entry retired (the duplicate render the container gate caught), and every workflow lane — the six build lanes beside the four publish lanes and the security lane the 26.7.0 pins carried — reads the one baseline the runtime policy pins across the engines, the nvmrc and the container image arg. |

### Fixed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| The verify lane red | The release candidate lane of 2.0.1 failed the api freeze step and the readiness review behind it; this bump re-synchronizes the freeze and the baseline so the gate ladder answers green end to end again. |
| The security posture | The one hundred open code scanning alerts of the merged repository answer the family policy: the ninety-six pinned-dependencies findings carry the documented version-tag contract dismissal (the maintenance ladder owns the update cycle, the version tag is the dependency contract of the family), the two cache-poisoning findings carry the release lockstep dismissal (the ref resolves under the tag-equals-head, the version lockstep and the changelog section gates — tags are cut by the bump loop alone), the best-practices badge finding carries the won't-fix (the security posture is the lane chain, not the badge), and the code-review finding closes through the branch protection of the main line the green cascade lands (the same doctrine the saddle line carries). |

## 2.0.1 — the grand merge made whole: every lost channel restored, the ladder lands, the suite answers green

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| The rich packaging channels | The 2.0.0 merge had replaced the extension's rich distribution with the simple DevThink adapters and left the packaging gates red. The single-distribution maven returns under the DevThink identity: io.github.wenathlan:devthink embeds the nine dist jar resources (index.js, index.cjs, devthink.umd.js, checksums.txt, cli.js, headless.js, mcp.js, gateway.js, http.js) and attaches the extension and the declarations zips as classifier artifacts through build-helper-maven-plugin, with distributionManagement pointing at the GitHub Packages registry of wenathlan/devthink. The NuGet channel restores the netstandard2.0;netstandard2.1 devthink.csproj with the twelve contentFiles entries (the zip, the cli/headless/mcp/umd/index.cjs entries, the declarations and the fixtures) beside the ExtensionDistribution identity class. The rubygems lane restores the workflow_run dual trigger with the tag-to-head and registry-existence guards. The mavenpack and nugetpack batteries gate the restored surfaces green. |
| THE Dockerfile | The 846-byte single-arch compile image becomes THE 620-line container file of the saddle pattern: the restored extension validation pipeline and self-hosting runner (the static site, the websocket relay and the loopback MCP child process) as the default stage, the binary runtime with the ARG TARGETARCH mapping (bun-linux-x64 and bun-linux-arm64 — the arm64 image stops shipping an x64 binary), the gateway-lineage smoke boot with the /healthz poll and the server-death detection, the non-root uid 10000, the OCI labels of wenathlan/devthink and the absorbed compose doctrine (read-only, cap-drop-all, no-new-privileges, the pids limit, the tmpfs, the memory engine and platform env) as documented docker run recipes. The compose.yml file itself is deleted — the rule: the container is managed only by the Dockerfile. |
| The release pipeline | The 4-job release lane grows back the eleven-job chain: the metadata lockstep with the CHANGELOG section contract, the verification ladder call, the assemble step (the full artifact chain from the chromium zip through the firefox xpi, the safari skeleton, the vsix, the site, the declarations, the umd pair, the nativehost template, the source archive and the artifact manifest), the per-channel verification jobs, the sbom and the attestation, the release assets with the SHA256SUMS umbrella, the npmjs lane with the flat distpackage, the provenance and the bounded retries, and the githubrelease job with the draft, the checksum-verified download and the live flip. The readiness gate answers go. |
| The maintenance ladder | The automated dependency ladder of the extension lineage returns adapted to the merged repository: the weekly and daily runs and the security-completion trigger apply every non-breaking npm update, keep the runtime engines current (node, npm, bun with the bounded forms; the pnpm lane follows the workflow pins while the root package manager stays bun), refresh the versioned action pins, derive the next rung with plain arithmetic over package.json, stamp the whole metadata family, run the full verification chain on an archive-tagged automation branch, squash the green result onto main and delete the branch — only main remains — and dispatch the release workflow on the new version tag. The registry gate learns the prerelease fallback: a release candidate riding the latest dist-tag (prisma ships them) never proposes; the ladder walks the published versions back to the newest stable. |
| The verification ladder | The verify lane builds the root distribution before the deterministic suite (the 2.0.0 red: the protocol battery read dist/schemas before any build produced it), restores the twenty-two-gate chain of the extension lane (the apifreeze, permdiff, pentest, cspaudit, agentcert, costcert, doccheck, sweep, poolaudit, matrixverify, telemetryfree, recipes, readiness, the mode matrix and the package verification), and the compatibility lane answers the node runtime through the built artifacts (node dist/cli.js — the source strip-types entry cannot resolve the merged .js specifiers) while the bun lane keeps the source contract. The seven maene and workbench batteries that spoke node:test join the vitest collection. |
| The library surface | The frozen library manifest of apifreeze grows by the twenty-six gateway vocabulary symbols the merge added to the shared types (authconfig through versionhandlers — the library surface of 2.0.0 is the merged surface), the freeze artifact re-freezes at the release, and the two lineage barrels ship as built entries: ./gateway-lib (the engine, the auth, the http, the configloader and the types of the gateway library) and ./maene-lib (the provider-neutral aggregates) — staged in the flat package beside every other entry, gated by the publishing battery. |
| The workflow family | The exchange with the saddle lineage lands: the codeql lane adopts the family style (the static language matrix, the concurrency group, the dispatch), the cache retention gains the scoped workflow_run pass and the java and dotnet families, the targets lane builds real plans from the artifact manifest with the channel matrix, the release sums umbrella rebuilds SHA256SUMS over the current assets with the idempotent re-run, the scorecard lane ships as its own weekly workflow, and the workflowlint pin follows the latest actionlint release. The governance files return: the merged CONTRIBUTING (the union of the three lineages' guides), the code of conduct and the CODEOWNERS. |
| The security posture | The five registry vulnerabilities of the merged lock close through the overrides block (deepmerge-ts 8.0.0, esbuild 0.28.2, mysql2 3.23.1, @xmldom/xmldom 0.9.12, qs 6.16.0) — the numbers only, the gates answer green. The SECURITY policy line moves to the 2.0.x release line. The reviewed provider endpoint catalog of the merged lineages becomes a data file (tests/artifacts/reviewedorigins.json) so the reviewed origins stay data rows, never code literals — the same doctrine the api coverage table carries. The one hundred twelve guarded best-effort catches of the maene lineage state their reason (the sweep doctrine: every catch surfaces its error or states its comment). |
| The metadata lockstep | The web manifest mirrors the root at 2.0.1 with the dependency set unified (the saddle doctrine: every root package lives at the web root and every web package lives at the root), the engines carry the bounded forms (node >=26.8.1 <27, npm >=12.0.2 <13, bun >=1.4.2 <2), the .nvmrc follows the node baseline, the sideEffects and the publish registry ride the package contract, the deno lane map returns (deno.json with the @wenathlan/devthink import), and the runtime baselines (node 26.8.1, npm 12.0.2, bun 1.4.2) land through the maintenance sync. |

### Fixed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| The 2.0.0 reds | The three merge commits never answered green: the CI lane red on the schema read before the build, the mavenpack and nugetpack batteries red on the replaced packaging, the node compatibility red on the rewritten import specifiers, the security lane red on the five registry vulnerabilities and the audit-gate capture, the secret-scan coverage and the verify dependencies. Every red closed at the root: the build order, the restored packaging, the artifact-based node contract, the number bumps and the gate wiring. |
| The build-heavy ceilings | The env-scaled ceilings of the library mode batteries follow the merged bundle sizes: the policy purity pass and the headless loads read the raised bounds (the doctrine of the 2.0.12 rung — a slow runner never fails a test that builds), and the suite cleanup hook carries the explicit bound the merged tree needs. |
| The generated output | The distpackage staging leaves the repository tree: the runners generate every build artifact (the flat package, the caps, the schemas, the zips) and the repository keeps no build output — the 2.0.0 contract finally holds. |
## 2.0.0 — the grand merge: gateway, extension and maene join devthink (one product, one binary, one metadata)

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| The grand merge | The gateway repository (private, v1.1.13 — the embedded provider gateway engine), the extension repository (v2.0.13 — the DevThink browser extension and agent surface) and the maene repository (private, v2.1.16 — the Antigravity engine) merge into devthink as one product. Every file, feature and document of the three repositories lands readapted to the DevThink metadata: the engine domains at the repository root (the family flat standard), the extension surfaces under web/extension/, the gateway console under web/gateway/, the full documentation sets under docs/ and the complete test suites under tests/. |
| The single binary | One bin — `devthink` routes every command family: the workbench commands stay top level (chat, providers, models, modes, config, auth, identity, pair, sync, sessions, projects, usage, routes, serve, interactive), the extension family rides `devthink ext <command>` (manifest, describe, planlint, migrateplan, recipes, flowrun, runworkflow, exportdata, headless, serve, native, doctor), the maene family rides `devthink maene <command>` (login, accounts, quota, config, models, status, doctor, menu) and the gateway family rides `devthink gateway <command>` (init, add, list, show, validate, keys, models, serve, export). The absorbed CLIs keep their argv surfaces through family wrappers — zero command loss. |
| The merged domains | Same-named domains merge as ordinal sections (config: the workbench config + the maene antigravity config + the gateway definition loader; memory and session: the extension domain with the workbench surface appended; streaming: the maene SSE machinery with the workbench ChatEvent family appended; types: the extension vocabulary with the gateway configuration vocabulary appended). Collision renames stay minimal and documented (agsaveConfig, gwauthmethod, the maene-*/antigravity file names). |
| Registry envelopes (the single metadata) | The devthink envelopes are THE metadata: DevThink.java and DevThinkCli.cs adopt the version-constant carrier (the gateway lineage pattern), devthink.csproj/devthink.gemspec/pom.xml carry 2.0.0 under GPL-3.0-only, and the gateway/extension/maene-named carriers stay in their archived repositories. The merged product publishes one artifact per registry under the DevThink names. |
| The one container file | The extension containerfile (the validation pipeline + the self-hosting runner with the websocket relay and the MCP child process) becomes THE Dockerfile of the merged repository, readapted to the merged dist layout. |
| License | The merged product rides GPL-3.0-only (the dominant lineage); the gateway, maene and devthink MIT histories are acknowledged in third-party-notices.md with their release histories preserved below. |
| Workflows | One workflow file per responsibility: the merged set inlines the retired composite actions (the saddle standard — the .github/actions folder no longer exists), carries the version-tag action pins, the event-scoped concurrency groups, the automated maintenance ladder (with the stale manifests pathspec fixed) and the full verification chain of the extension lane (apifreeze, permdiff, pentest, cspaudit, agentcert, costcert, doccheck, sweep, poolaudit, matrixverify, telemetryfree, recipes, readiness, chromium smoke, the mode matrix). |
| Build artifacts | Nothing generated lives in the repository: dist, distpackage, the sqlite stores and every build cache stay out (the runners generate everything). |

## 1.1.16 — Browser-local data foundation
### Added
| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| Browser-local workbench data | The static workbench now uses a native IndexedDB database named `devthink.db` for non-sensitive workspaces, sessions, tabs, messages and interface preferences. |
| Identity-scoped records | Browser-local records include public owner ID, browser device ID, update time, revision and tombstone state to prepare explicit future synchronization without transferring raw SQLite files. |
| Offline fallback | Home, Projects, Usage, Providers and Settings retain browser-local state when the paired CLI gateway is unavailable, while a successful paired read refreshes the local cache. |
| Sync state | Settings now distinguishes `local-only` browser persistence from the paired local gateway and makes clear that a remote adapter is optional and not configured. |
| Credential boundary | Provider selection is a non-secret local preference. Credentials remain CLI-owned by default; an explicit opt-in may save one in a separate browser-only vault that is never displayed, gatewayed or synchronized. |
| Public maintenance skills | The public `wenathlan/skills` repository now includes DevThink-specific skills for architecture, local data, paired gateway and release workflows. |

## 1.1.15 — Unified capabilities and Settings
### Added
| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| Capability matrix | A renderer-neutral matrix now maps every safe local action across CLI commands, Ink destinations, authenticated gateway routes and web pages. |
| Shared Settings page | `/settings` is a dedicated browser page equivalent to `devthink config settings`, `devthink config set` and the Ink Settings view. |
| Settings summary | The authenticated `GET /settings` route returns identity, pairing session count, preferences, active provider summary and local database ownership metadata without secrets or filesystem paths. |
| Same database client | The paired browser operates as a temporary client of the CLI-owned `devthink.db` for one public ID; it does not create an independent static database. |
| Settings domain | Pairing controls now belong to `web/settings/`, and the common control navigation includes Settings alongside Providers, Projects, Routes and Usage. |

## 1.1.14 — Route and local identity refinement

### Added

| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| Portable Home route | Relative static builds preserve the Vite `./` base and resolve the Home route at `/` without treating `.` as a router prefix. |
| CLI-owned person | The browser now projects the authenticated CLI-owned public user and device identity after pairing instead of storing a second browser identity. |
| Shared public ID | `devthink identity --id <value>` and the paired web Settings panel update the same local public identifier, with compact 10–15 character validation. |
| Shared route index | Web and CLI route maps now list the authenticated `GET` and `PUT /identity` operations alongside preferences and workspace routes. |
| Documentation boundary | Workbench design and deployment notes moved from `/web` to `docs/`, leaving the static application tree free of planning documents. |

## 1.1.13 — Flat domain consolidation

### Added

| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| Flat application entry | `web/App.tsx` now owns React mounting, routing, the error boundary, global stylesheet import and browser notification surface; `main.tsx` is no longer required. |
| Domain-only web tree | Generic `app`, `contexts`, `hooks`, `lib`, `primitives`, `public`, `components`, `pages` and `src` directories have been removed from the workbench. |
| Local ownership | Page-level controls now use native semantic elements, while the Routes map owns its only callback and class-join helpers directly. |
| Compact visual grammar | Historical workspace CSS overrides were replaced with one terminal workspace system for entry, tabs, rail, command bar, pairing, controls and error states. |
| Control frame | Providers, projects, routes and usage share one explicitly named root control shell without a generic application directory. |
| Preserved parity boundary | Web and Ink retain the same safe workspace destinations, settings and local gateway interactions; provider credentials remain CLI-owned. |
| Portable Home route | Relative Vite builds now retain `./` as a relative base and normalize it to the Home router base, so `/` resolves the first workbench screen in portable static artifacts. |
| Same local person | A paired browser reads the CLI-owned public user and device IDs through the authenticated gateway instead of creating a second browser profile. |
| Public ID controls | `devthink identity --id <value>` and the paired web Settings panel update the same local public ID, preserving compact workspace data and temporary browser sessions. |
| Documentation boundary | Workbench deployment and design records now live under `docs/`, leaving `/web` exclusively for browser application artifacts. |

## 1.1.12 — Page domains and tabbed interaction

### Added

| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| Page-domain topology | The web workbench now keeps each route anchor and its local visual logic in its named domain, with `home`, `providers`, `projects`, `routes`, `usage`, `notfound`, `app` and `primitives` replacing generic `components` and `pages` directories. |
| Canonical local database | DevThink now uses `~/.config/devthink/devthink.db` as the canonical SQLite path and safely seeds it from the legacy `devthink.sqlite` file when needed. |
| Shared workbench preferences | Theme, rail mode and interface zoom are stored in the CLI-owned database, exposed only through authenticated local gateway endpoints, and configurable with `devthink config set`. |
| Interactive Settings parity | The web Settings panel and Ink `/settings` view can both inspect and change shared presentation preferences without receiving provider credentials. |
| Tab-first workspace frame | The workbench promotes the browser-like tab row as its primary navigation and permits an `always`, `auto` or `off` local navigation rail. |
| Consolidated identity | The entry screen centers the product wordmark over the canonical ANSI mark while the workspace keeps the rail as its single optional identity surface. |
| Mutation boundary | JSON-bearing gateway mutations now require an explicit JSON content type; pairing, bearer authorization and local-only provider credential boundaries remain intact. |

## 1.1.11 — Web-CLI parity and visual renewal

### Added

| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| Shared workspace contract | A renderer-neutral TypeScript destination model aligns chat, history, projects, providers, usage, routes and settings across the React web workbench and React Ink CLI. |
| Real session navigation | The web chrome now renders actual local session tabs, creates and closes tabs, and exposes a local history view without removing direct routes. |
| CLI control views | The interactive CLI now provides keyboard and slash-command access to local history, projects, providers, usage, routes and settings. |
| Browser rail | A responsive dark local rail keeps preserved control pages discoverable from the primary workspace while retaining their bookmarkable routes. |
| Entry refinement | The canonical ANSI mark, product wordmark, primary composer and local suggestions form a quieter first-intention surface. |
| Design records | Version-scoped inventory, reference synthesis and parity contract documents make the product surface and local-only security boundary explicit. |

## 1.1.10 — Entry and browser-workspace redesign

### Added

| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| Landing entry | A first-command introduction with the canonical DevThink ANSI mark behind an oversized wordmark and one focused command field. |
| First-command transition | A submitted natural-language intention creates a local identity, records the first session intent and opens the workspace. |
| Browser workspace | A two-level navigation model with browser-shaped session chrome, an active local tab, task-category pills and a persistent command rail. |
| Visual system | Original black editorial surfaces, layered charcoal browser material, intentional orange states and orb-blue local connection signals. |
| CLI parity | The Ink renderer now starts on the same command-first entry surface and opens the same local workspace semantics after the first command. |
| Release packaging | Locked CLI dependencies are installed before Bun binary compilation and inside the container build so the React Ink renderer is included consistently. |

## The gateway lineage (the merged engine history, preserved)

## 1.1.13 — The publication lane that cancelled the validation lane

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| Every release left a cancelled red-X run in the actions tab | The `publish ghcr` workflow grouped its runs by ref alone (`publish-ghcr-refs/heads/main`), so the version-bump push started the per-arch validation legs and then, 60–90 seconds later, the Release workflow completed and fired the same workflow through `workflow_run` into the SAME concurrency group with `cancel-in-progress` on — the publication run killed the in-flight validation legs and the actions tab showed a cancelled run under every release since the container pipeline landed. The group is scoped by event now (`publish-ghcr-${{ github.event_name }}-${{ github.ref }}`): the push validation run and the workflow_run publication run for the same commit run side by side and both finish green, while every dedupe guarantee the ref-only group carried survives — duplicate `workflow_run` firings for the same release completion still cancel each other instead of racing the GHCR publish, superseded push validations still cancel each other on rapid double-pushes, and publication runs (`release`, `workflow_dispatch`) are never cancelled mid-flight because a latecomer queues behind them. No hashes or checksums were introduced anywhere in the fix — the gate hygiene stays generated, never hardcoded. |
| The push validation never actually completed on a release push | A side effect of the same collision: because every push to main in this repository is a version bump, the validation legs (the amd64 and arm64 dry-run builds with the boot probe) were cancelled mid-build on essentially every push they were meant to serve — the per-arch server-contract check had not survived to completion since the container pipeline landed. With the lanes separated, both legs now build, boot the image, and probe `/api/config` on every push to main and complete green — on non-release pushes (which never fire the publication lane) they are the only container gate that runs, and on release pushes they run alongside the scan/publish/verify chain, which independently validates the same commit end to end. |

## 1.1.12 — The heuristic that kept one more key in its pocket

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| The container scan skipped its Trivy legs one more run | The 1.1.11 fix cured the healthcheck and two of the three false keys, but the first post-fix security run still failed the dockle gate on two new reveals: the smoke-boot RUN's own `node -e` poll expression carried the same arrow callbacks (`r=>{...}`) as the healthcheck did — one more `=` in the image history text, one more manufactured "suspicious ENV key" — and the node slim base layer's install RUN hides a whole family of `=`-bearing tokens behind the first one (`dpkgArch` was already whitelisted; accepting it revealed `ARCH`, behind which wait `OPENSSL_ARCH`, `GNUPGHOME`, `--strip-components`, the awk classifier's `/`, and the apt purge option key — the scanner reports only the first unaccepted match per layer, so each accepted key peels the gate failure back to the next). The smoke-boot poll is now written with `function(r){}` callbacks and no `=` character — same poll, same 30s ceiling, same semantics, nothing left for the heuristic to split — and the accept-key list carries the full node-layer set, every one of them base image internals (architecture mappings, a gpg temp home, a tar flag, the dependency classifier, an apt knob; no secret material anywhere in the list). The gate exits clean and the Trivy SARIF scan, the code scanning upload, and the critical-CVE gate run on every security run. |

## 1.1.11 — The security gate that skipped its own scans

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| The container scan was skipping its Trivy legs | The dockle step in the security workflow failed with a CIS-DI-0010 FATAL on three non-secrets — the smoke-boot loop's `waited` counter, the node base layer's `dpkgArch` probe, and the healthcheck's own inline expression — and because a failed step ends the job, every step after it (`Scan image with Trivy (SARIF report)`, `Upload Trivy report to code scanning`, `Gate on critical vulnerabilities`) had been silently skipped since the smoke-boot landed in 1.1.10: the image's SARIF never reached code scanning and the critical-CVE gate never evaluated, while the job's informational `continue-on-error` kept the workflow green so nothing surfaced. The gate passes for real now and the legs run on every security run. |
| The healthcheck tripped the credential heuristic | Buildkit records `HEALTHCHECK` into the image config history as text, and the CIS-DI-0010 heuristic splits any token containing `=` on that character to invent a candidate credential pair — the old healthcheck's arrow callbacks (`r=>{...}`) put a `=` right in the recorded text, so the scanner derived a "suspicious ENV key" out of `fetch(...).then(r` and demanded a whitelist entry for a string that isn't even a variable name. The healthcheck expression is now written with `function(r){}` callbacks and contains no `=` character anywhere, which the heuristic cannot parse into a pair at all — the same check with the same semantics (200 from `/api/config` exits 0, anything else exits 1), no fragile string-matching whitelist entry to maintain. |
| The accept-key list covered only one of three pseudo-variables | `serverpid` (the smoke-boot's background server pid) was already whitelisted, but the same RUN's `waited` retry counter and the node slim base image's `dpkgArch` architecture probe hit the identical heuristic — and the scanner reports only the first unaccepted match per layer, so whitelisting one just revealed the next. All three are process state and build internals, never secrets; the list now carries all of them with the reasoning in place. |

### Housekeeping

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| Code scanning triaged to zero | All 100 open code scanning alerts were OSSF Scorecard `Pinned-Dependencies` findings against the deliberate version-tag pinning policy (actions and base images pinned by tag per the family standard — extension, e2ugh, maene, saddle, devthink — with digests and checksums generated at run time by the operations that produce them, never hardcoded). Each alert was resolved with a documented maintainer-policy dismissal — 98 "won't fix" for the tag pins and base image refs, 2 "used in tests" for the consumer-e2e job's `npm install` of the locally packed tarball — and a fresh security workflow run confirmed the dismissals hold: zero open alerts with the full pipeline (CodeQL, Trivy filesystem + container, Dockle, TruffleHog, OSV, Scorecard, Biome, SBOM, license compliance) green. |

## 1.1.10 — The real consumer run

### Added

| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| The consumer run | The library is now verified the way a first-time user actually installs it: `npm pack` the tarball, `npm init -y` a fresh directory (no `type: module` — the npm default), install the tarball, import the library from plain node with a mock upstream, scaffold with piped `gateway init` answers, `prisma db push && generate`, `serve`, hit every route over real HTTP (config, catalog, chat, stream, CORS, console, hashed asset, SPA, 404), and read the rows back out of the pushed database — then clean up. The CI gained a `consumer` job that runs the same flow on every push: pack, install, plain-node library smoke (`scripts/consumer-smoke.mjs`), piped cli scaffold, prisma push + generate + persistence smoke. |
| Persistence suite | `tests/persistence.test.ts` pushes a real sqlite per run and proves the engine write path (a `handlechatcompletions` call lands a full row: content, model, provider, version, session, Date-typed `startedat`, token counts) plus the history read path the rotation and context features depend on (status filter, oldest-first order, limit). |
| 348 tests | The suite grew from 334 to 348: persistence (8), `.mjs` config loading (2), single-terminator stream regressions (3, plus the strict count assertion in the e2e stream test — every previous assertion used `toContain`, which is why a duplicated marker shipped for eleven releases). |

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| The scaffold crashed plain-node consumers | `init` scaffolded `web/config.ts` with ESM syntax — under plain node the module type of a `.ts`/`.js` file follows the nearest `package.json`, and a stock `npm init -y` project has no `type: module`, so loading the scaffolded config died with `Failed to load the ES module` and every subsequent command answered `no config found`. The scaffold is now `web/config.mjs` — the extension carries the module type, immune to the package flavor — and both the library config loader and the cli search `.mjs` first (`.ts`/`.js` still load for existing projects); `add` writes back to whichever file was found and `export` copies it under its own name. |
| Every terminated stream carried two done markers | `extractdata` maps the done marker to `null` by contract — so in the stream loop the marker fell into the "no data lines" branch, the terminated flag never set, and the loop-end `if (!closed)` appended a second `data: [DONE]`. An unterminated trailing marker duplicated the same way through the residual flush. The loop (and the flush) now recognize the marker from the raw frame payload before `extractdata` nulls it: exactly one terminator, everywhere, including lowercase `[done]` upstreams. |
| File database overrides were silently ignored | `resolvedburl` only honored env urls with remote prefixes — a `GATEWAY_DATABASE_URL=file:...` (the documented local flow in `.env.example`) fell through to the hardcoded default path. The resolver now honors the gateway's own variable for every url flavor, and treats a local `file:` `DATABASE_URL` as the host's database (never adopted — the gateway keeps its own sqlite), while remote `DATABASE_URL`s still pass through as the deployment convention. |
| Engine writes never landed in the database | `persist()` passed `startedat` as epoch millis into a `DateTime` column — validation failed inside the silent catch, so every engine write no-oped (the consumer run read back zero rows). The wrapper now coerces the timestamp to a `Date` at the single choke point. |
| The persist contract drifted from both schemas | The engine persists `version`, `endpoint`, `twocalls` and `thinkingbudget`; the cli template lacked the last three (and its `createdAt` spelling broke the history-restore `orderBy createdat` for every scaffolded consumer), while the repo schema lacked `version` and `twocalls`. Both schemas now match the engine field set exactly, all lowercase per the code convention. |
| ensuredb cjs interop | A commonjs `@prisma/client` resolution exposes the class on the default export — the named destructuring could yield `undefined` and the constructor call died inside the silent catch. Both spellings are accepted now, with a real error message when neither resolves. |

## 1.1.9 — The aggressive test suite

### Added

| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| 334 tests, 9 files | The suite grew from 13 placeholder/property tests to 334 across 9 files: `utils` (every helper pushed through NaN, Infinity, numeric strings, circular structures, bigints, surrogate pairs, concurrency), `authentication` (the 12 auth methods, every header position, key resolution from env/inline/mixed sources, round robin, query-param building and encoding), `http` (the stream pipeline against mock upstreams — frame splitting, heartbeat discard, masking, tracking, residual flush, mid-stream errors, bodyless responses, keepalive, unicode round trips, ndjson byte passthrough), `engine` (the 7 handlers end-to-end against a live mock upstream server — happy paths, paused, 401 key enforcement, retry, rotation on status and per session, timeouts, format conversion for completions/messages/responses, embeddings, malformed bodies), `configloader` (loading, caching, reload, every validation problem class), `server.e2e` (the real server spawned as a child process with a temp config dir over real HTTP: every route, CORS preflight, 404s, 30 parallel requests), `cli` (init with piped answers, list, show, validate, models, keys, export, help, unknown commands — the subprocess consumer flow). |
| Zero-400 contract | Malformed JSON bodies and missing/empty message arrays now answer `400 invalid_request` on all four action routes — previously the engine forwarded the empty request upstream and surfaced a confusing error far from the real cause. |

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| Streamed responses crashed library consumers | `makestreamresponse` enqueued **string** chunks into the `ReadableStream` it wraps in `new Response()` — reading that body with `res.text()`/`res.json()` threw `TypeError: Received non-Uint8Array chunk` (node http writes happened to tolerate strings, which is why the console path looked fine while every library embedding of the stream utils crashed). Every chunk is encoded to bytes now, and the pure passthrough branch forwards the raw upstream bytes with no decode/encode round trip. |
| Bodyless upstream streams never terminated | An upstream `Response(null)` closed the downstream stream without the `data: [DONE]` marker — consumers parsing for the terminator hung until their own timeout. The no-body branch emits the marker before closing. |
| validateconfig crashed on broken configs | A version with a missing `auth` block (or missing `models` with rotation/defaultmodel set) threw a `TypeError` instead of reporting the problem list — the validator's whole purpose. All dereferences are guarded; broken configs now validate to readable problems. |
| Rotation statuses hijacked from fallback | The status classifier defaulted the rotate set to `[529, 404, 410]` whenever `retry.maxretries` was set — so an explicit `fallbackstatuses: [404]` classified 404 as "rotate" and the cross-provider swap never ran. The rotate set now only comes from an explicit `rotation.rotateonstatus`; rotation is declared, never inferred from retry settings. |
| Empty-data heartbeats leaked downstream | `discardheartbeat` compared the trimmed line against `"data: "` — impossible after `trim()` removes the trailing space, so empty data keepalive lines were forwarded instead of discarded (dead comparison since the line was written). |
| clamp treated Infinity as NaN | `clamp(Infinity, 0, 10)` returned the **fallback** instead of the bound — only NaN falls back now; ±Infinity clamps to the max/min bound like every other out-of-range value. |
| autoseed rounded negatives inconsistently | `autoseed(-12.9)` produced 13 while `autoseed(12.9)` produced 12 — floor ran before abs, rounding negatives away from zero. Abs runs first now: both produce 12. |
| CLI colors polluted piped output | The cli emitted ANSI escapes unconditionally — `gateway export | grep` matched nothing because color bytes sat mid-string. Colors are now gated on `stdout.isTTY`. |
| Session ids from bodies ignored | The handlers called `extractsessionid(req)` without the parsed body, so a `session_id`/`sessionId` in the request body never reached the session store (only the header worked). The body is passed on every route. |
| engineinternals.buildbody | The `model` parameter is optional now (callers without a resolved model fall back to `cfg.defaultmodel` then the meta id) — matches how the engine itself calls it and how tests exercise it. |

## 1.1.8 — The real consumer flow

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| The library import crashed fresh consumers | `npx @wenathlan/gateway init` — the documented quick start — crashed before printing a single prompt: `database.ts` instantiated the prisma singleton at module scope, so merely importing the library loaded `@prisma/client`, whose `default.js` requires the **generated** client — which a fresh consumer does not have yet (the very thing `init` exists to bootstrap). The database is lazy now: a path proxy resolves every `db.model.method` chain through a dynamic `import("@prisma/client")` on the first database touch, so the library and the whole cli import prisma-free until persistence is actually used. |
| The prisma 7 scaffold | `init` now scaffolds `prisma.config.ts` (the datasource url lives there since prisma 7 — the old template carried a `url` inside the schema file, which prisma 7 rejects outright: `The datasource property url is no longer supported in schema files`), the schema template drops the url, and the printed next steps name the real sequence (`npm i -D prisma`, `npx prisma db push && npx prisma generate`). The config default and the library runtime fallback are the same `file:./prisma/devthink.db`, so the pushed database and the serving process agree on one file with zero configuration. |
| Piped stdin lost between prompts | The cli opened a new readline interface per question — closing the first dropped every buffered answer (piped/automation runs died at the second prompt with EOF). One interface now serves the whole command and queues lines that arrive while no question is pending; interactive typing is unchanged, end-of-input answers the remaining questions with their defaults. |
| The console out of the box | `serve` served the static console from `dist` relative to the **cwd** — a consumer with no local build got a bare 204 on `/`. The static roots now fall back to the console that ships inside the package (the bundled `dist/http.js` sits next to it): a host build still wins, `npx @wenathlan/gateway serve` serves the console out of the box, and the SPA fallback actually returns `index.html` (the comment always claimed it; the code used to answer 204). |
| Real-consumer verification | The whole flow — install from the registry, `init`, `npx prisma db push && npx prisma generate`, `serve`, then `/api/config`, the console page, a hashed asset and an SPA route — runs against the published package in a fresh directory before release, plus the repo smoke boot (server, config endpoint, console, hashed asset) on the built bundle. |

## 1.1.7 — Generated, not hardcoded

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| No hardcoded hashes in the workflows | Every `uses:` ref across the twelve workflow files is converted from a 40-hex commit-SHA pin to its version tag (94 pins — the same family standard `extension`, `e2ugh`, `maene`, `saddle` and `devthink` pin by). A hash literal in a workflow file cannot track what the operation resolves: the release `SHA256SUMS` are computed by `sha256sum` over the artifacts the build just produced, the image digest comes from the registry push output, and the action version comes from the tag ref the workflow names — nothing hash-shaped lives as a literal in a file. |
| Base images by tag, not digest | The Dockerfile `BUN_IMAGE`/`NODE_IMAGE` ARGs drop the `@sha256:` digest pins — a single hardcoded digest cannot serve both multi-arch legs (the digest varies with the platform), and the registry resolves the tag to the right per-platform digest at pull time. Pinning the tree is the lockfiles' job (`bun.lock` + committed `package-lock.json`, `npm ci --omit=dev`), not the base image reference's. |
| osv-scanner.toml deleted | The deepmerge advisory (`GHSA-ggr8-5vv4-36mx` / `CVE-2026-40345`, stack exhaustion) is now resolved for real instead of documented: an `overrides` pin (`deepmerge-ts 8.0.2`) replaces the 7.1.5 the prisma CLI dev-tool chain declared (the `deepmerge` API the config loader calls is stable across the major; `prisma generate`, the test suite and both builds verify). Both lockfiles resolve the tree clean, the osv job carries no ignore list, and `.trivyignore.yaml` is deleted with it — the filesystem, container and release trivy gates now hold on every finding they report with zero ignore files. |
| Metadata lockstep | `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `pom.xml`, `gateway.csproj`, `gateway.gemspec`, the Dockerfile version label, the java and c# cli version constants, the README release line and the interface tree doc all carry 1.1.7 in lockstep. |

## 1.1.6 — The fresh advisory and the label heuristic

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| mysql2 advisory resolved for real | `GHSA-rgwj-5xj2-c3m3` (MySQL2 unbounded zlib inflate, decompression-bomb DoS — published the same day) reaches the repository only through the prisma CLI dev-tool chain, which pins mysql2 exactly at 3.15.3. The fix is an npm/bun `overrides` pin (`mysql2 ^3.23.1`) that resolves the advisory everywhere: both lockfiles now carry mysql2 3.24.2, osv-scanner and the trivy filesystem gate report clean, and the runtime tree is untouched (mysql2 never ships in the image — `npm ci --omit=dev` excludes the prisma CLI chain; every runtime dependency version is unchanged). |
| Dockle gate on the new family label | The merged-in `com.wenathlan.gateway.image="library"` label tripped the dockle CIS-DI-0010 "suspicious ENV key" heuristic (FATAL). The key joins the documented accept-key list — it is the gateway family label with the literal value `library`, no secret material. |
| Code-scanning board cleared | The seven historical alerts are dismissed through the delegated-dismissal flow with approved review comments: the four `js/insecure-randomness` orphans (stale instances of the deleted pre-1.0.0 tree — the code has routed through `securerandom` since 1.0.0) and the three scorecard policy alerts (Maintained: repository younger than the 90-day window; CodeReview: direct-push history predates the ruleset that now enforces reviews; CIIBestPractices: external badge signup). The 1.1.5 scan's `VulnerabilitiesID` + two `GHSA-rgwj-5xj2-c3m3` alerts close with the mysql2 fix above. |

## 1.1.5 — One container file

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| One container file | `docker-compose.yml` is merged INTO the `Dockerfile` and deleted — one file owns the whole container context (Containerfile is the same format under the OCI name; Dockerfile is the universally compatible spelling, so it is the file the repository keeps). Every compose setting lives in the Dockerfile: ENV defaults, EXPOSE, the new `VOLUME /data`, the HEALTHCHECK, the non-root USER, the OCI LABELs (plus the former compose label `com.wenathlan.gateway.image=library`) and the hardened `docker run` line documenting every orchestration flag compose used to carry (init, cap-drop ALL, no-new-privileges, ulimits, unlimited swap, 2g shm, 15s stop grace, json-file logs 50m×5, restart unless-stopped, host port and named volume). |
| Build cache embedded in the gateway image | The separate `gateway-buildcache` container image is gone — the durable registry cache is the **inline cache embedded in the published gateway image itself** (`cache-to: type=inline` on the publish push, `cache-from: ghcr.io/wenathlan/gateway:<version>` on every leg). The scan/validate legs write only run-scoped gha caches that their cleanup jobs already delete. No `-buildcache` image is pushed by any workflow ever again. |
| Release container bundle | The release artifact ships the one container file (Dockerfile + .dockerignore) instead of Dockerfile + compose; the bundle README carries the docker run line. |
| Envelope gate | The CI `envelopes` gate and the release `metadata` job now verify the version through the Dockerfile `ARG GATEWAY_VERSION` default (the former `docker-compose.yml` image-tag check) — the lockstep contract is unchanged, the carrier is the one container file. |
| Commit identity | Repository commits are authored as `iakadion` (the account that owns the publish token) instead of the sandbox default. |

## 1.1.4 — One context one file

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| One owner per logic | The dead devthink meta-model helpers (`devthinkid`, `devthinkcontext`, `devthinkmaxoutput`, `devthinkbase`) and the duplicated `intelligencerank` interface are removed from `utils.ts` — they were leftovers of the deleted `gatewayvN.ts` tree, and the meta-model identity context now lives exactly once: the type surface in `types.ts` (`intelligencerank`, `modeldef.rank`) and the user definitions in `web/config.ts`. `utils.ts` is purely the shared-helpers file again — one context, one file, no logic duplicated across files. |
| Metadata lockstep | `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `pom.xml`, `gateway.csproj`, `gateway.gemspec`, `docker-compose.yml`, the Dockerfile version label, the java and c# cli version constants, the README release line and the interface tree doc all carry 1.1.4 in lockstep — the envelope gate (CI) and the metadata job (release) verify every one of them on each push. |

> Note: the v1.1.3 ref name landed in GitHub's deleted-ref quarantine (GH013)
> when its release was deleted for a re-run, which blocked the tag push that
> fires the publish chain for every token; the registry family (npmjs,
> GitHub npm, Maven, NuGet, RubyGems, GHCR) first ships complete again at
> 1.1.4, which carries the whole 1.1.3 merged tree.

## 1.1.3 — The one simple container and the merged tree

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| One simple container | `Dockerfile.full` (the VM-style drivers image) and `entrypoint.sh` are merged away — the repository now ships ONE simple container (stencil standard: a container serves one thing well) built from the single `Dockerfile` (builder / deps / runtime, node 26 slim, non-root). The compose stack collapses to one `gateway` service with one image tag and one named volume. |
| HTTP transport merge | `server.ts` and `streaming.ts` were the same transport context (http server + http stream) split across two files; they are now embedded in one `http.ts` (0–100 correlated logics per file). The public API surface is unchanged — everything still re-exports from the package root, and the `./server` subpath becomes `./http`. |
| Design lives in web/ | The interface folder is the deploy-ready design unit: `index.html`, `vite.config.ts`, `vite-env.d.ts`, `vercel.json` and `netlify.toml` moved into `web/` next to `app.tsx`, `config.ts`, `globals.css` and `schema.prisma`; a new `web/capacitor.config.ts` wraps the same interface for Android. Everything design is in one folder, everything library logic stays at the root. |
| Build cache inside the gateway pipeline | The standalone cache-retention workflow is merged into the maintenance workflow's retention job (the gateway build cache lifecycle lives inside the gateway's own workflows — there is no separate cache file). The merged job keeps the scoped workflow_run protection: security-workflow completions only clean the codeql family, never the buildkit/node/trivy families an in-flight container leg may be reading. |
| GHCR publish profile | The publish pipeline builds and ships exactly one multi-arch image (linux/amd64 + linux/arm64) with the single version tag; the full-profile validation/publish/verify legs are gone with Dockerfile.full. |
| Pinned workflow actions | Every `uses:` across all workflows is pinned by commit SHA with the version tag in a comment (the OpenSSF Pinned-Dependencies contract) — resolves the 98 code-scanning `Pinned-Dependencies` alerts. The dependabot bumps (setup-java 6.0.0, sbom-action 0.24.2, trufflehog 3.97.1, biome 2.5.11) are folded into the same change. |
| Runtime deps pinning | The container deps stage installs from the committed `package-lock.json` (`npm ci --omit=dev`) — zero floating resolution at build time; the lockfile is checked in next to `bun.lock` (bun stays the package manager of record for the builder). |
| Correlated scripts merged | The three gh-auth helpers (daemon / pexpect / pty) are one `scripts/gh_auth.py` with a `--mode` flag; the three deploy scripts are one `scripts/deploy.sh [vercel\|netlify]` — one file per context, no variations. The stale build artifact `dist-cli-test.js` is removed from the tree. |
| netlify manifest | The `netlify.toml` header table was malformed (`[eaders]]`); it is fixed and the manifest now lives in `web/` with the interface it deploys. |

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| Stale code-scanning alerts | The four `js/insecure-randomness` alerts pointed at the pre-1.0.0 tree (Math.random); the current sources already route every security-sensitive path through `securerandom()` (node:crypto randomInt), and the refreshed analysis confirms it. |

## 1.1.2 — Production container tree

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| Runtime dependency tree | The container images carried the whole prisma CLI chain: `@prisma/client` declares `prisma` and `typescript` as peer dependencies, so npm auto-installed peers (and bun's production filter leaked the same tree through the frozen lockfile) — shipping the TypeScript 7 native Go compiler, Prisma Studio's react stack and the deepmerge advisory into the runtime image. The production tree is now resolved with `npm ci --omit=dev --legacy-peer-deps` (25 packages, every runtime transport present) and the trivy release gate scans clean. |

## 1.1.1 — Container family hardening (historical)

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| Full image healthcheck | The VM-style image healthcheck used the compose `CMD-SHELL` spelling, which the Dockerfile parser rejects; it now uses the Dockerfile shell form. |
| Full image fonts | Ubuntu noble ships the color emoji font as `fonts-noto-color-emoji` (with `fonts-noto-cjk` for east-asian rendering); `fonts-noto-color` is not a noble binary package and broke the apt layer. |
| Full image chromium path | Playwright 1.62 downloads Chrome for Testing with the `chrome-linux64` directory layout; the chromium symlink glob now matches both the legacy `chrome-linux` and the current layout. |
| Driver version probes | The validation probes pipe multi-line version output through `head -n1`, which raises SIGPIPE (exit 141) under `pipefail` when the pipe closes early; the probes tolerate the broken pipe. |
| Maintenance proposal | The maintenance proposal is scoped to npm surfaces (package.json, the lockfile, .nvmrc): the job token cannot push workflow-file changes, so GitHub Actions reference updates are owned by dependabot. |
| Release tag automation | A workflow_dispatch from the default branch also creates the missing version tag (tag-protection rulesets block human tag pushes; the job token creates them through the contents write permission). |
| Platform surface | Both container profiles build on linux/amd64 and linux/arm64 — the platform surface the bun builder image actually ships; the workflow documents the constraint instead of failing on exotic architectures. |
| Shellcheck cleanups | Derived docker container names and the envelopes version interpolation are quoted; the flat-layout report line carries an explicit shellcheck justification. |

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| Library bundle | The npm package ships a real dist bundle: the esm entries, the shebang-corrected cli and the tsc declarations built by `scripts/build-lib.mjs`; the config loader probes the module parent directory so packaged installs resolve `web/config.ts`, and the cli resolves the version from the parent package.json. |

> Note: 1.1.1 shipped the library to every registry but its GHCR gate
> failed on the leaked dev toolchain; the container family first ships
> complete at 1.1.2.

## 1.1.0 — Release engineering foundation (registry-only historical)

### Added

| Area | Addition |
| --- | --- |
| Release pipeline | A release pipeline adapted from the wenathlan reference family: pushing a bumped package.json version to main creates the `v{version}` tag automatically, extracts the release notes from this changelog, and attaches a maximum-compression `zip -9` source archive with SHA-256 checksums together with the container bundle assets. |
| Publish chain | Every release publishes the library to npmjs.com, GitHub Packages npm, Maven, NuGet and RubyGems, and publishes the container images to GHCR: the light library image and the full VM-style driver image. |
| Security pipeline | CodeQL analysis (javascript and actions), dependency review, OSV scanning, Trivy filesystem and container scans, a TruffleHog git history scan, OSSF Scorecard and a CycloneDX SBOM guard every change. |
| Governance documents | SECURITY.md, CODEOWNERS, the code of conduct and the contributing guide document the supported release line, repository ownership and the contribution contract. |
| Maintenance automation | A maintenance workflow proposes dependency and GitHub Actions reference updates through reviewable pull requests; routine runs apply non-breaking updates only and major updates require an explicitly authorized manual run. |
| Support gates | A cache retention workflow keeps the Actions cache store bounded per family, and a workflow lint gate runs actionlint over every workflow change. |
| Container family | A light library image, a full VM-style driver image with the browser driver stack, and compose orchestration for running the stack. |

### Changed

| Area | Change |
| --- | --- |
| Lint gate | Biome 2.5.10 replaces ESLint as the lint gate: `npm run lint` now runs `biome check .` with `lint:fix` and `format` scripts alongside, and the flat `biome.json` configuration carries the repository conventions. |
| Tooling pins | Continuous integration and maintenance run on Node 26.7.0 with bun 1.4.0 for lockfile refreshes, matching the repository `.nvmrc` and `packageManager` field. |

### Fixed

| Area | Fix |
| --- | --- |
| Secure randomness | Cryptographically secure randomness (`randomInt` from `node:crypto`) replaces `Math.random` in every security-sensitive path — backoff jitter, session identifiers and id generation — closing the four code-scanning high alerts. |
| Web console | The conditional React hook in the web console config view became a proper component, so the React hook rules hold on every render path. |

> Note: the 1.1.0 tag was tombstoned after its release was retracted to
> re-ship the container fixes; the registry artifacts at 1.1.0 (npmjs,
> GitHub Packages, Maven, NuGet, RubyGems) remain valid library builds.
